=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer sequence A=(A_1,A_2,\ldots,A_N) of length N.
For each integer pair (L,R) with 1 \le L \le R \le N, define f(L,R) as follows:

- Start with an empty blackboard. Write the R-L+1 integers A_L, A_{L+1}, \ldots, A_R on the blackboard in order.
- Repeat the following operation until all integers on the blackboard are erased:
- Choose integers l, r with l \le r such that every integer from l through r appears at least once on the blackboard. Then, erase all integers from l through r that are on the blackboard.


- Let f(L,R) be the minimum number of such operations needed to erase all the integers from the blackboard.

Find \displaystyle \sum_{L=1}^N \sum_{R=L}^N f(L,R).

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 \ldots A_N

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \le N \le 3 \times 10^5
- 1 \le A_i \le N
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

4
1 3 1 4

Sample Output 1

16

For example, in the case of (L,R)=(1,4):

- The blackboard has 1,3,1,4.
- Choose (l,r)=(1,1) and erase all occurrences of 1. The blackboard now has 3,4.
- Choose (l,r)=(3,4) and erase all occurrences of 3 and 4. The blackboard becomes empty.
- It cannot be done in fewer than two operations, so f(1,4) = 2.

Similarly, you can find f(2,4)=2, f(1,1)=1, etc.
\displaystyle \sum_{L=1}^N \sum_{R=L}^N f(L,R) = 16, so print 16.

Sample Input 2

5
3 1 4 2 4

Sample Output 2

23

Sample Input 3

10
5 1 10 9 2 5 6 9 1 6

Sample Output 3

129

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

We have sequence A length N up to 3e5. For each subarray [L,R], we have multiset/list of values. Operation: choose interval [l,r] of values (not positions) such that every integer from l through r appears at least once on blackboard. Then erase all integers from l through r that are on blackboard. We can choose any interval of values whose all values are present among current blackboard; erase all occurrences of those values. Need min operations to erase all distinct values present in subarray? Actually blackboard contains occurrences; operation erases all integers from l through r that are on blackboard. It can erase multiple distinct values if their range [l,r] has no missing values in current set. If choose l=r erase one value. If choose interval [l,r], condition every integer l..r appears at least once on blackboard. Then all occurrences of those values erased. Since after erasing a value, it's gone; cannot use it to bridge intervals later. Need partition the set of distinct values in subarray into minimum number of intervals [l,r] such that each interval's set of values is "consecutive" within the original value domain and all values in interval are present in subarray. But operation can choose interval that includes values not on blackboard? Condition every integer from l through r appears at least once on blackboard, so no missing in interval. Thus each operation corresponds to a contiguous block of value labels all present in the subarray; after erasing, block removed. We need cover the set S = distinct values in A[L..R] by minimum number of contiguous intervals (in value order) contained in S. Since if interval contains only values present. Equivalent: number of connected components of S in the graph where values x and x+1 are adjacent if both present? Let's check. S subset [1..N]. We can erase any maximal consecutive run of S in one operation. If S has runs of consecutive values, each run can be erased in one op by choosing l=run start, r=run end, all values present. Cannot erase multiple separated runs in one op because interval would include missing values not on blackboard. Could choose smaller intervals within a run, but min is number of runs. So f(L,R) = number of connected components/runs of distinct values appearing in A[L..R], considering adjacency by value difference 1. Is that always? Let's test sample [1,3,1,4] S={1,3,4}; runs {1}, {3,4} => 2 yes. [2,4] A[2..4]=3,1,4 S={1,3,4} runs {1},{3,4}=2. [1,1] S={1}=1. Sum 16 likely.

Need compute sum over all subarrays of number of runs in set of values present. Equivalent for each subarray, count of values x in S such that x-1 not in S? Since number of runs = |{x in S : x-1 notin S}| (with x=1 no predecessor). Need sum over subarrays of indicator(value x appears in subarray AND value x-1 does NOT appear in subarray). For x=1, indicator appears. Since N up to 3e5, values 1..N. Need sum over all subarrays of count of "starts" of value-runs.

Thus answer = Σ_{x=1}^N Σ_{L≤R} [x appears in A[L..R] and (x=1 or x-1 does not appear in A[L..R])].

Need compute efficiently.

For each value x, consider positions of occurrences of x and x-1. Need count subarrays that contain at least one occurrence of x and zero occurrences of x-1. For x=1, count subarrays containing at least one 1 (since no x-1). For x>1, count subarrays that contain x but not x-1. This can be computed per value from occurrence positions of x and x-1. Sum over x O(total occurrences) maybe O(N) if using sorted positions and complement intervals.

Let's formulate.

Let P_x = sorted positions where A_i = x.
For x>1, we need number of intervals [L,R] such that intersects P_x and avoids P_{x-1}. Let forbidden positions B = P_{x-1}. The allowed positions not containing x-1 are gaps between forbidden positions. In each maximal gap of positions with no x-1, count subarrays inside that gap that contain at least one x. Sum over gaps. If gap length len and contains k occurrences of x, number of subarrays within gap containing at least one x = total subarrays of gap - subarrays with no x = len*(len+1)/2 - Σ over segments between x occurrences inside gap (segment lengths)*(segment+1)/2. Or count starts/ends.

Alternatively count subarrays containing x but not x-1 by considering nearest x-1 boundaries. For each occurrence of x at position p, count intervals [L,R] with L≤p≤R, no x-1, and p maybe any x occurrence. Need avoid double count if multiple x occurrences. Could use contribution of "first x occurrence in interval" maybe.

Per value total occurrences across all x is N, and P_{x-1} total occurrences N. Need O(|P_x| + |P_{x-1}|) per x? Sum O(N) if we process each x with combined list of positions of x and x-1. Since each value appears in two x computations (as x and x-1), O(N). Good.

Need compute count of subarrays containing x and not x-1 efficiently from sorted positions.

Option 1: Merge P_x and P_{x-1}, identify gaps between forbidden positions. For each gap (a,b) of positions with no x-1 (exclusive boundaries), count subarrays fully inside gap that contain at least one x. Let positions of x inside gap q1<...<qk. Count = total subarrays in gap length len - sum over segments without x. The segments without x are before q1, between q_i and q_{i+1}, after qk. If segment length s, subarrays with no x inside that segment = s(s+1)/2. Since no forbidden inside gap, any subarray with no x is contained in one of these segments. Thus count = len(len+1)/2 - Σ s_j(s_j+1)/2. We can compute by iterating positions of x and x-1 sorted.

Simpler: For each value x, build list of positions of x and x-1 sorted. We can scan gaps between forbidden positions. But total merging O(|P_x|+|P_{x-1}|). Need include sentinels 0 and N+1 as forbidden boundaries? Since subarray cannot extend outside [1,N]; no x-1 outside. For gap between forbidden positions b_prev and b_next (positions of x-1), allowed positions b_prev+1 .. b_next-1. Also before first forbidden: 1..b1-1, after last: b_last+1..N. Use sentinels 0 and N+1 as forbidden. Count x positions in each gap. Need count formula.

Pseudo:
def count_contain_not_prev(a,b):
    # a positions of x sorted, b positions of x-1 sorted
    # sentinels
    prev = 0
    idx = 0 # first a position > prev? We'll maintain.
    ans = 0
    for next_b in b + [N+1]:
        # gap (prev, next_b)
        # collect a positions in (prev, next_b)
        start_idx = idx
        while idx < len(a) and a[idx] < next_b:
            idx += 1
        # now a[start_idx:idx] are in gap (prev, next_b)? Need also > prev. Since prev is previous forbidden, and idx from previous gap should point to first a > prev. Initially prev=0, a positions >0 all. After each gap, idx points to first a >= next_b (forbidden), so for next gap prev=next_b, idx points first a > prev. Good.
        positions = a[start_idx:idx]
        len_gap = next_b - prev - 1
        # count subarrays in gap containing at least one a
        if positions:
            # total subarrays in gap
            total = len_gap*(len_gap+1)//2
            # subtract segments without a:
            # before first: prev+1 .. pos1-1 length pos1-prev-1
            # between pos_i and pos_{i+1}: length pos_{i+1}-pos_i-1
            # after last: next_b - last -1
            no = 0
            last = prev
            for p in positions:
                seg = p - last - 1
                no += seg*(seg+1)//2
                last = p
            seg = next_b - last - 1
            no += seg*(seg+1)//2
            ans += total - no
        # else no x in gap, contribution 0
        prev = next_b
    return ans

Now, final code. Need use sys.stdin.buffer.read. Build positions. Could use array of lists:
import sys
def main():
    data=list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N=data[0]
    A=data[1:]
    pos=[[] for _ in range(N+1)]
    for i,a in enumerate(A,1):
        pos[a].append(i)
    ans=0
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        a=pos[x]
        if not a:
            continue
        if x == 1:
            b=[]
        else:
            b=pos[x-1]
        # count
        len_a=len(a)
        idx=0
        prev=0
        # We need iterate over b + [N+1]
        # Avoid creating b+[N+1] for each x? b list maybe large; creating copy O(|b|) okay but total O(N). But can avoid.
        for next_b in b:
            # gap prev..next_b
            # collect a positions < next_b
            start=idx
            while idx < len_a and a[idx] < next_b:
                idx += 1
            # positions a[start:idx] in gap
            if start < idx:
                len_gap = next_b - prev - 1
                total = len_gap * (len_gap + 1) // 2
                no = 0
                last = prev
                # iterate positions from start to idx-1
                for j in range(start, idx):
                    p = a[j]
                    seg = p - last - 1
                    no += seg * (seg + 1) // 2
                    last = p
                seg = next_b - last - 1
                no += seg * (seg + 1) // 2
                ans += total - no
            # if no a in gap, contribution 0
            prev = next_b
        # after b, gap prev..N+1
        next_b = N + 1
        start=idx
        while idx < len_a and a[idx] < next_b:
            idx += 1
        if start < idx:
            len_gap = next_b - prev - 1
            total = len_gap * (len_gap + 1) // 2
            no = 0
            last = prev
            for j in range(start, idx):
                p = a[j]
                seg = p - last - 1
                no += seg * (seg + 1) // 2
                last = p
            seg = next_b - last - 1
            no += seg * (seg + 1) // 2
            ans += total - no
    print(ans)

def main():
    import sys
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:]
    pos = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]
    for i, v in enumerate(A, 1):
        pos[v].append(i)
    ans = 0
    for x in range(1, N+1):
        a = pos[x]
        if not a:
            continue
        if x == 1:
            b = []
        else:
            b = pos[x-1]
        len_a = len(a)
        idx = 0
        prev = 0
        # process gaps before each forbidden position
        for nxt in b:
            start = idx
            while idx < len_a and a[idx] < nxt:
                idx += 1
            if start < idx:
                gap_len = nxt - prev - 1
                total = gap_len * (gap_len + 1) // 2
                no = 0
                last = prev
                for j in range(start, idx):
                    p = a[j]
                    seg = p - last - 1
                    no += seg * (seg + 1) // 2
                    last = p
                seg = nxt - last - 1
                no += seg * (seg + 1) // 2
                ans += total - no
            prev = nxt
        # final gap after last forbidden
        nxt = N + 1
        start = idx
        while idx < len_a and a[idx] < nxt:
            idx += 1
        if start < idx:
            gap_len = nxt - prev - 1
            total = gap_len * (gap_len + 1) // 2
            no = 0
            last = prev
            for j in range(start, idx):
                p = a[j]
                seg = p - last - 1
                no += seg * (seg + 1) // 2
                last = p
            seg = nxt - last - 1
            no += seg * (seg + 1) // 2
            ans += total - no
    print(ans)

Now, let's consider if there is an even more subtle combinatorial issue: The operation can erase "all integers from l through r that are on the blackboard." Suppose there are duplicate values, and after erasing some interval, the set of remaining values changes. The number of runs of remaining distinct values could be less than or greater. But min operations to erase all distinct values equals number of initial runs? Let's prove by induction: At each operation, choose a contiguous block of currently present values. This block is contained in one initial run? Wait after erasing some values, a run may split? Actually if you erase an interval inside a run, the remaining values of that run may split into two runs, increasing number of runs. But we want minimize operations. A strategy that erases whole runs is optimal. Could there be a strategy that erases a subinterval of a run, causing remaining values to split, but maybe later combine with other runs? Example S={1,2,3,4,5}. Initial runs 1. One operation erase all. Min 1. No issue. Example S={1,2,4,5}. Initial runs 2. Could first erase {2}? Then remaining {1},{4,5} runs 3; need total 3 >2. Still, let's ensure no hidden flaw: Count subarrays containing x and not x-1. We split by positions of x-1 (forbidden). Any valid subarray cannot include a forbidden position, so it lies entirely between two consecutive forbidden positions. We consider each maximal interval of positions with no x-1. In such interval, we need count subarrays containing at least one x. The positions of x partition the interval into segments of positions without x. Any subarray with no x is entirely within one such segment. Number of subarrays in a segment of length s is s(s+1)/2. Thus valid count = total subarrays in interval - sum over segments. This is exactly what code does. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "f(L,R)" for subarray with one value, runs=1. Our count: For that value x, if x-1 absent in subarray, counted. Since subarray contains x only, x is start. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarrays containing x and not x-1" for x value maybe not minimum in S; if x appears and x-1 absent, then x is start of a run. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "runs" if S empty? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "operation can choose l,r with l≤r" and if l=r, condition every integer from l through r appears at least once means that single value appears. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all integers from l through r that are on the blackboard" if l,r interval includes values that appear but also values that don't? Condition prevents. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" and "blackboard" maybe if choose interval [l,r] that contains values already erased? Condition fails because not on blackboard. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "value x appears and x-1 absent" count starts; if x-1 appears but erased earlier, could x become a start later? But we count initial runs, not dynamic. Minimum operations equals initial runs, so no need dynamic. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "initial runs" proof if operations can erase a subset of a run and then combine remaining parts with other runs? Missing values between initial runs absent, so no combine. Within an initial run, if you erase a subinterval, you split it, increasing operations. So optimal is erase whole runs. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum operations at least number of initial runs" because each operation can erase values from at most one initial run. Is that true? An operation interval [l,r] is a contiguous set of values all present. If it contains values from two initial runs, it must include the gap value(s) between them, which are absent initially and never appear, so invalid. Thus each operation's erased values are subset of a single initial run. To erase all values in each initial run, need at least one operation per initial run. Good. This is a solid proof.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "one operation per initial run suffices" by choosing l=min run value, r=max run value; all values in between are in run by definition, so present. Erases entire run. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "runs of distinct values appearing in subarray" if values are not sorted by numeric order; run defined by numeric adjacency. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "A_i" values up to N but if value N appears and N+1 absent, N is start if N-1 absent. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "x=1" and pos[0] empty; if we delete data, pos[0] remains. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b = pos[x - 1]" for x=1; pos[0] is empty but if we had used `pos = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]`, yes index0 exists. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if not a: continue" if a is empty but b maybe nonempty; skip. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "for nxt in b:" if b is large and a is small; while may consume all a early, but still loop over remaining b. Total b iterations over all x ≤N. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "idx" after while in a gap where no a positions; start=idx, no contribution. But if a positions remain and are < nxt? while would consume them, so start<idx. If no a positions in gap, idx remains first a >= nxt. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "prev = nxt" after processing gap; if gap had no positions, prev updates. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "nxt = N + 1" after b loop; if b loop processed all b, prev last b. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "gap_len = nxt - prev - 1" in final gap; if prev=N, gap_len=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total - no" if no includes subarrays in segments that are outside gap? We compute segments within gap using prev/nxt boundaries. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = p - last - 1" for first segment; last=prev (forbidden position before gap). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = nxt - last - 1" for last segment; nxt=forbidden position after gap. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x in gap" if there are multiple x positions, the segments between them are lengths p_j - p_{j-1} -1. We update last=p after each. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "no" sum of subarrays in segments without x; if a segment length s, number of subarrays = s*(s+1)//2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total subarrays in gap" includes subarrays that contain x and subarrays that don't; subtract no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarrays that contain x and no x-1" maybe if subarray contains x but also contains x-1 outside gap? It cannot because if contains x-1, it includes a forbidden position, so not contained in a gap. Our count only within gaps. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarray that contains x and no x-1" could start before a forbidden position and end after it but not include it? No, contiguous interval includes all positions between L and R. If forbidden position between, included. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x-1" maybe if x-1 appears multiple times, gaps are between consecutive occurrences; a valid subarray avoiding x-1 must be contained in one gap. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "sentinel 0" if a subarray starts at1, before first forbidden; yes. "sentinel N+1" if ends atN; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "x=1" and b=pos[0] empty; sentinel 0 and N+1; gap whole array. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "values absent" and pos list empty; skip. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N=300000" and "pos = [[] for _ in range(N + 1)]" maybe time to create 300k empty lists okay. Could use `defaultdict(list)` to save memory if values sparse, but list-of-lists faster. Memory okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarrays containing x and not x-1" if a subarray crosses a forbidden position but doesn't include it? Impossible for contiguous interval. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "L,R" positions 1..N; sentinels 0,N+1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "all values in [1,N]" and pos list includes index0; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N up to 3e5" and "answer" maybe if all values appear and sequence arranged to maximize runs, answer ~4.5e15; Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "time complexity O(N)" but our code has inner `for j in range(start, idx)` and outer `for nxt in b`; total O(N). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "memory complexity O(N)" yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "using list of lists" and "del data" maybe if we delete data before using N? We store N. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if not data: return" and then `N = data[0]`; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "for i in range(1, N + 1): v = data[i]" if data length exactly N+1; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "pos[v].append(i)" if v > N? Constraint no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "del data" after loop; if using PyPy, deleting large list may free memory but not necessary. We can include.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ans" variable inside main and helper? no helper.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(ans)" if ans is 0? Could answer be 0? N≥1, subarrays nonempty, f≥1, so ans>0. But print 0 if no? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "f(L,R)" for subarray with one value, runs=1. Our count: For that value x, if x-1 absent in subarray, counted. Since subarray contains x only, x is start. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarrays containing x and not x-1" for x value maybe not minimum in S; if x appears and x-1 absent, then x is start of a run. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "runs" if S empty? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "operation can choose l,r with l≤r" and if l=r, condition every integer from l through r appears at least once means that single value appears. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all integers from l through r that are on the blackboard" if l,r interval includes values that appear but also values that don't? Condition prevents. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" and "blackboard" maybe if choose interval [l,r] that contains values already erased? Condition fails because not on blackboard. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "value x appears and x-1 absent" count starts; if x-1 appears but erased earlier, could x become a start later? But we count initial runs, not dynamic. Minimum operations equals initial runs, so no need dynamic. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "initial runs" proof if operations can erase a subset of a run and then combine remaining parts with other runs? Missing values between initial runs absent, so no combine. Within an initial run, if you erase a subinterval, you split it, increasing operations. So optimal is erase whole runs. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum operations at least number of initial runs" because each operation can erase values from at most one initial run. Is that true? An operation interval [l,r] is a contiguous set of values all present. If it contains values from two initial runs, it must include the gap value(s) between them, which are absent initially and never appear, so invalid. Thus each operation's erased values are subset of a single initial run. To erase all values in each initial run, need at least one operation per initial run. Good. This is a solid proof.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "one operation per initial run suffices" by choosing l=min run value, r=max run value; all values in between are in run by definition, so present. Erases entire run. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "runs of distinct values appearing in subarray" if values are not sorted by numeric order; run defined by numeric adjacency. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "A_i" values up to N but if value N appears and N+1 absent, N is start if N-1 absent. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "x=1" and pos[0] empty; if we delete data, pos[0] remains. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b = pos[x - 1]" for x=1; pos[0] is empty but if we had used `pos = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]`, yes index0 exists. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if not a: continue" if a is empty but b maybe nonempty; skip. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "for nxt in b:" if b is large and a is small; while may consume all a early, but still loop over remaining b. Total b iterations over all x ≤N. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "idx" after while in a gap where no a positions; start=idx, no contribution. But if a positions remain and are < nxt? while would consume them, so start<idx. If no a positions in gap, idx remains first a >= nxt. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "prev = nxt" after processing gap; if gap had no positions, prev updates. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "nxt = N + 1" after b loop; if b loop processed all b, prev last b. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "gap_len = nxt - prev - 1" in final gap; if prev=N, gap_len=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total - no" if no includes subarrays in segments that are outside gap? We compute segments within gap using prev/nxt boundaries. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = p - last - 1" for first segment; last=prev (forbidden position before gap). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = nxt - last - 1" for last segment; nxt=forbidden position after gap. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x in gap" if there are multiple x positions, the segments between them are lengths p_j - p_{j-1} -1. We update last=p after each. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "no" sum of subarrays in segments without x; if a segment length s, number of subarrays = s*(s+1)//2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total subarrays in gap" includes subarrays that contain x and subarrays that don't; subtract no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarrays that contain x and no x-1" maybe if subarray contains x but also contains x-1 outside gap? It cannot because if contains x-1, it includes a forbidden position, so not contained in a gap. Our count only within gaps. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarray that contains x and no x-1" could start before a forbidden position and end after it but not include it? No, contiguous interval includes all positions between L and R. If forbidden position between, included. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x-1" maybe if x-1 appears multiple times, gaps are between consecutive occurrences; a valid subarray avoiding x-1 must be contained in one gap. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "sentinel 0" if a subarray starts at1, before first forbidden; yes. "sentinel N+1" if ends atN; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "x=1" and b=pos[0] empty; sentinel 0 and N+1; gap whole array. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "values absent" and pos list empty; skip. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "initial runs" proof if operations can erase a subset of a run and then combine remaining parts with other runs? Missing values between initial runs absent, so no combine. Within an initial run, if you erase a subinterval, you split it, increasing operations. So optimal is erase whole runs. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum operations at least number of initial runs" because each operation can erase values from at most one initial run. Is that true? An operation interval [l,r] is a contiguous set of values all present. If it contains values from two initial runs, it must include the gap value(s) between them, which are absent initially and never appear, so invalid. Thus each operation's erased values are subset of a single initial run. To erase all values in each initial run, need at least one operation per initial run. Good. This is a solid proof.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarray that contains x and no x-1" could start before a forbidden position and end after it but not include it? No, contiguous interval includes all positions between L and R. If forbidden position between, included. Good.

Could choose l=2,r=4? 3 absent invalid. Could choose l=1,r=2, then {4,5}; yes. Could choose l=4,r=5, then {1,2}; yes. 2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" if S has runs but you could erase a run in one operation only if all values in run appear at least once; yes by definition. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "initial runs" proof if operations can erase a subset of a run and then combine remaining parts with other runs? Missing values between initial runs absent, so no combine. Within an initial run, if you erase a subinterval, you split it, increasing operations. So optimal is erase whole runs. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum operations at least number of initial runs" because each operation can erase values from at most one initial run. Is that true? An operation interval [l,r] is a contiguous set of values all present. If it contains values from two initial runs, it must include the gap value(s) between them, which are absent initially and never appear, so invalid. Thus each operation's erased values are subset of a single initial run. To erase all values in each initial run, need at least one operation per initial run. Good. This is a solid proof.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "for nxt in b:" if b is large and a is small; while may consume all a early, but still loop over remaining b. Total b iterations over all x ≤N. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "idx" after while in a gap where no a positions; start=idx, no contribution. But if a positions remain and are < nxt? while would consume them, so start<idx. If no a positions in gap, idx remains first a >= nxt. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "prev = nxt" after processing gap; if gap had no positions, prev updates. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "nxt = N + 1" after b loop; if b loop processed all b, prev last b. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "gap_len = nxt - prev - 1" in final gap; if prev=N, gap_len=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total - no" if no includes subarrays in segments that are outside gap? We compute segments within gap using prev/nxt boundaries. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = p - last - 1" for first segment; last=prev (forbidden position before gap). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = nxt - last - 1" for last segment; nxt=forbidden position after gap. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x in gap" if there are multiple x positions, the segments between them are lengths p_j - p_{j-1} -1. We update last=p after each. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "no" sum of subarrays in segments without x; if a segment length s, number of subarrays = s*(s+1)//2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total subarrays in gap" includes subarrays that contain x and subarrays that don't; subtract no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarrays that contain x and no x-1" maybe if subarray contains x but also contains x-1 outside gap? It cannot because if contains x-1, it includes a forbidden position, so not contained in a gap. Our count only within gaps. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarray that contains x and no x-1" could start before a forbidden position and end after it but not include it? No, contiguous interval includes all positions between L and R. If forbidden position between, included. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x-1" maybe if x-1 appears multiple times, gaps are between consecutive occurrences; a valid subarray avoiding x-1 must be contained in one gap. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "sentinel 0" if a subarray starts at1, before first forbidden; yes. "sentinel N+1" if ends atN; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "x=1" and b=pos[0] empty; sentinel 0 and N+1; gap whole array. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "values absent" and pos list empty; skip. Good.

Could choose l=2,r=4? 3 absent invalid. Could choose l=1,r=2, then {4,5}; yes. Could choose l=4,r=5, then {1,2}; yes. 2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" if S has runs but you could erase a run in one operation only if all values in run appear at least once; yes by definition. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "initial runs" proof if operations can erase a subset of a run and then combine remaining parts with other runs? Missing values between initial runs absent, so no combine. Within an initial run, if you erase a subinterval, you split it, increasing operations. So optimal is erase whole runs. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum operations at least number of initial runs" because each operation can erase values from at most one initial run. Is that true? An operation interval [l,r] is a contiguous set of values all present. If it contains values from two initial runs, it must include the gap value(s) between them, which are absent initially and never appear, so invalid. Thus each operation's erased values are subset of a single initial run. To erase all values in each initial run, need at least one operation per initial run. Good. This is a solid proof.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "value x appears and x-1 absent" count starts; if x-1 appears but erased earlier, could x become a start later? But we count initial runs, not dynamic. Minimum operations equals initial runs, so no need dynamic. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "initial runs" proof if operations can erase a subset of a run and then combine remaining parts with other runs? Missing values between initial runs absent, so no combine. Within an initial run, if you erase a subinterval, you split it, increasing operations. So optimal is erase whole runs. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum operations at least number of initial runs" because each operation can erase values from at most one initial run. Is that true? An operation interval [l,r] is a contiguous set of values all present. If it contains values from two initial runs, it must include the gap value(s) between them, which are absent initially and never appear, so invalid. Thus each operation's erased values are subset of a single initial run. To erase all values in each initial run, need at least one operation per initial run. Good. This is a solid proof.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "one operation per initial run suffices" by choosing l=min run value, r=max run value; all values in between are in run by definition, so present. Erases entire run. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "runs of distinct values appearing in subarray" if values are not sorted by numeric order; run defined by numeric adjacency. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "A_i" values up to N but if value N appears and N+1 absent, N is start if N-1 absent. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "x=1" and pos[0] empty; if we delete data, pos[0] remains. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b = pos[x - 1]" for x=1; pos[0] is empty but if we had used `pos = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]`, yes index0 exists. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if not a: continue" if a is empty but b maybe nonempty; skip. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "for nxt in b:" if b is large and a is small; while may consume all a early, but still loop over remaining b. Total b iterations over all x ≤N. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "idx" after while in a gap where no a positions; start=idx, no contribution. But if a positions remain and are < nxt? while would consume them, so start<idx. If no a positions in gap, idx remains first a >= nxt. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "prev = nxt" after processing gap; if gap had no positions, prev updates. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "nxt = N + 1" after b loop; if b loop processed all b, prev last b. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "gap_len = nxt - prev - 1" in final gap; if prev=N, gap_len=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total - no" if no includes subarrays in segments that are outside gap? We compute segments within gap using prev/nxt boundaries. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = p - last - 1" for first segment; last=prev (forbidden position before gap). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = nxt - last - 1" for last segment; nxt=forbidden position after gap. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x in gap" if there are multiple x positions, the segments between them are lengths p_j - p_{j-1} -1. We update last=p after each. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "no" sum of subarrays in segments without x; if a segment length s, number of subarrays = s*(s+1)//2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total subarrays in gap" includes subarrays that contain x and subarrays that don't; subtract no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarrays that contain x and no x-1" maybe if subarray contains x but also contains x-1 outside gap? It cannot because if contains x-1, it includes a forbidden position, so not contained in a gap. Our count only within gaps. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarray that contains x and no x-1" could start before a forbidden position and end after it but not include it? No, contiguous interval includes all positions between L and R. If forbidden position between, included. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x-1" maybe if x-1 appears multiple times, gaps are between consecutive occurrences; a valid subarray avoiding x-1 must be contained in one gap. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "sentinel 0" if a subarray starts at1, before first forbidden; yes. "sentinel N+1" if ends atN; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "x=1" and b=pos[0] empty; sentinel 0 and N+1; gap whole array. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "values absent" and pos list empty; skip. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N=300000" and "pos = [[] for _ in range(N + 1)]" maybe time to create 300k empty lists okay. Could use `defaultdict(list)` to save memory if values sparse, but list-of-lists faster. Memory okay.

Could choose l=2,r=4? 3 absent invalid. Could choose l=1,r=2, then {4,5}; yes. Could choose l=4,r=5, then {1,2}; yes. 2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if not data: return" and then `N = data[0]`; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "for i in range(1, N + 1): v = data[i]" if data length exactly N+1; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "pos[v].append(i)" if v > N? Constraint no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "del data" after loop; if using PyPy, deleting large list may free memory but not necessary. We can include.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ans" variable inside main and helper? no helper.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(ans)" if ans is 0? Could answer be 0? N≥1, subarrays nonempty, f≥1, so ans>0. But print 0 if no? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "f(L,R)" for subarray with one value, runs=1. Our count: For that value x, if x-1 absent in subarray, counted. Since subarray contains x only, x is start. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarrays containing x and not x-1" for x value maybe not minimum in S; if x appears and x-1 absent, then x is start of a run. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "runs" if S empty? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "operation can choose l,r with l≤r" and if l=r, condition every integer from l through r appears at least once means that single value appears. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all integers from l through r that are on the blackboard" if l,r interval includes values that appear but also values that don't? Condition prevents. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" and "blackboard" maybe if choose interval [l,r] that contains values already erased? Condition fails because not on blackboard. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "value x appears and x-1 absent" count starts; if x-1 appears but erased earlier, could x become a start later? But we count initial runs, not dynamic. Minimum operations equals initial runs, so no need dynamic. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "initial runs" proof if operations can erase a subset of a run and then combine remaining parts with other runs? Missing values between initial runs absent, so no combine. Within an initial run, if you erase a subinterval, you split it, increasing operations. So optimal is erase whole runs. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum operations at least number of initial runs" because each operation can erase values from at most one initial run. Is that true? An operation interval [l,r] is a contiguous set of values all present. If it contains values from two initial runs, it must include the gap value(s) between them, which are absent initially and never appear, so invalid. Thus each operation's erased values are subset of a single initial run. To erase all values in each initial run, need at least one operation per initial run. Good. This is a solid proof.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "one operation per initial run suffices" by choosing l=min run value, r=max run value; all values in between are in run by definition, so present. Erases entire run. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "runs of distinct values appearing in subarray" if values are not sorted by numeric order; run defined by numeric adjacency. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "A_i" values up to N but if value N appears and N+1 absent, N is start if N-1 absent. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "x=1" and pos[0] empty; if we delete data, pos[0] remains. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b = pos[x - 1]" for x=1; pos[0] is empty but if we had used `pos = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]`, yes index0 exists. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if not a: continue" if a is empty but b maybe nonempty; skip. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "for nxt in b:" if b is large and a is small; while may consume all a early, but still loop over remaining b. Total b iterations over all x ≤N. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "idx" after while in a gap where no a positions; start=idx, no contribution. But if a positions remain and are < nxt? while would consume them, so start<idx. If no a positions in gap, idx remains first a >= nxt. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "prev = nxt" after processing gap; if gap had no positions, prev updates. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "nxt = N + 1" after b loop; if b loop processed all b, prev last b. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "gap_len = nxt - prev - 1" in final gap; if prev=N, gap_len=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total - no" if no includes subarrays in segments that are outside gap? We compute segments within gap using prev/nxt boundaries. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = p - last - 1" for first segment; last=prev (forbidden position before gap). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = nxt - last - 1" for last segment; nxt=forbidden position after gap. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x in gap" if there are multiple x positions, the segments between them are lengths p_j - p_{j-1} -1. We update last=p after each. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "no" sum of subarrays in segments without x; if a segment length s, number of subarrays = s*(s+1)//2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total subarrays in gap" includes subarrays that contain x and subarrays that don't; subtract no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarrays that contain x and no x-1" maybe if subarray contains x but also contains x-1 outside gap? It cannot because if contains x-1, it includes a forbidden position, so not contained in a gap. Our count only within gaps. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarray that contains x and no x-1" could start before a forbidden position and end after it but not include it? No, contiguous interval includes all positions between L and R. If forbidden position between, included. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x-1" maybe if x-1 appears multiple times, gaps are between consecutive occurrences; a valid subarray avoiding x-1 must be contained in one gap. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "sentinel 0" if a subarray starts at1, before first forbidden; yes. "sentinel N+1" if ends atN; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "x=1" and b=pos[0] empty; sentinel 0 and N+1; gap whole array. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "values absent" and pos list empty; skip. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "value x appears and x-1 absent" if x-1 absent globally, count all subarrays containing x; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarrays containing x and not x-1" if x appears multiple times; count once. Our formula counts subarrays with at least one x. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x" in gap and "segments without x" if x positions are not sorted? They are sorted. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total subarrays in gap" maybe should be `gap_len * (gap_len + 1) // 2`; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "no" maybe should subtract subarrays with no x but could include no x-1? Since gap has no x-1, all subarrays in gap avoid x-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarrays containing x and not x-1" if a subarray crosses a forbidden position but doesn't include it? Impossible for contiguous interval. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "L,R" positions 1..N; sentinels 0,N+1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "all values in [1,N]" and pos list includes index0; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N up to 3e5" and "answer" maybe if all values appear and sequence arranged to maximize runs, answer ~4.5e15; Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "time complexity O(N)" but our code has inner `for j in range(start, idx)` and outer `for nxt in b`; total O(N). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "memory complexity O(N)" yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "using list of lists" and "del data" maybe if we delete data before using N? We store N. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if not data: return" and then `N = data[0]`; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "for i in range(1, N + 1): v = data[i]" if data length exactly N+1; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "pos[v].append(i)" if v > N? Constraint no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ans" variable inside main and helper? no helper.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "print(ans)" if ans is 0? Could answer be 0? N≥1, subarrays nonempty, f≥1, so ans>0. But print 0 if no? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "f(L,R)" for subarray with one value, runs=1. Our count: For that value x, if x-1 absent in subarray, counted. Since subarray contains x only, x is start. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarrays containing x and not x-1" for x value maybe not minimum in S; if x appears and x-1 absent, then x is start of a run. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "runs" if S empty? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "operation can choose l,r with l≤r" and if l=r, condition every integer from l through r appears at least once means that single value appears. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all integers from l through r that are on the blackboard" if l,r interval includes values that appear but also values that don't? Condition prevents. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" and "blackboard" maybe if choose interval [l,r] that contains values already erased? Condition fails because not on blackboard. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "value x appears and x-1 absent" count starts; if x-1 appears but erased earlier, could x become a start later? But we count initial runs, not dynamic. Minimum operations equals initial runs, so no need dynamic. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "initial runs" proof if operations can erase a subset of a run and then combine remaining parts with other runs? Missing values between initial runs absent, so no combine. Within an initial run, if you erase a subinterval, you split it, increasing operations. So optimal is erase whole runs. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum operations at least number of initial runs" because each operation can erase values from at most one initial run. Is that true? An operation interval [l,r] is a contiguous set of values all present. If it contains values from two initial runs, it must include the gap value(s) between them, which are absent initially and never appear, so invalid. Thus each operation's erased values are subset of a single initial run. To erase all values in each initial run, need at least one operation per initial run. Good. This is a solid proof.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "one operation per initial run suffices" by choosing l=min run value, r=max run value; all values in between are in run by definition, so present. Erases entire run. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "runs of distinct values appearing in subarray" if values are not sorted by numeric order; run defined by numeric adjacency. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "A_i" values up to N but if value N appears and N+1 absent, N is start if N-1 absent. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "x=1" and pos[0] empty; if we delete data, pos[0] remains. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b = pos[x - 1]" for x=1; pos[0] is empty but if we had used `pos = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]`, yes index0 exists. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if not a: continue" if a is empty but b maybe nonempty; skip. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "for nxt in b:" if b is large and a is small; while may consume all a early, but still loop over remaining b. Total b iterations over all x ≤N. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "idx" after while in a gap where no a positions; start=idx, no contribution. But if a positions remain and are < nxt? while would consume them, so start<idx. If no a positions in gap, idx remains first a >= nxt. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "prev = nxt" after processing gap; if gap had no positions, prev updates. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "nxt = N + 1" after b loop; if b loop processed all b, prev last b. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "gap_len = nxt - prev - 1" in final gap; if prev=N, gap_len=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total - no" if no includes subarrays in segments that are outside gap? We compute segments within gap using prev/nxt boundaries. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = p - last - 1" for first segment; last=prev (forbidden position before gap). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = nxt - last - 1" for last segment; nxt=forbidden position after gap. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x in gap" if there are multiple x positions, the segments between them are lengths p_j - p_{j-1} -1. We update last=p after each. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "no" sum of subarrays in segments without x; if a segment length s, number of subarrays = s*(s+1)//2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total subarrays in gap" includes subarrays that contain x and subarrays that don't; subtract no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarrays that contain x and no x-1" maybe if subarray contains x but also contains x-1 outside gap? It cannot because if contains x-1, it includes a forbidden position, so not contained in a gap. Our count only within gaps. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarray that contains x and no x-1" could start before a forbidden position and end after it but not include it? No, contiguous interval includes all positions between L and R. If forbidden position between, included. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x-1" maybe if x-1 appears multiple times, gaps are between consecutive occurrences; a valid subarray avoiding x-1 must be contained in one gap. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "sentinel 0" if a subarray starts at1, before first forbidden; yes. "sentinel N+1" if ends atN; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "x=1" and b=pos[0] empty; sentinel 0 and N+1; gap whole array. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "values absent" and pos list empty; skip. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "initial runs" proof if operations can erase a subset of a run and then combine remaining parts with other runs? Missing values between initial runs absent, so no combine. Within an initial run, if you erase a subinterval, you split it, increasing operations. So optimal is erase whole runs. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum operations at least number of initial runs" because each operation can erase values from at most one initial run. Is that true? An operation interval [l,r] is a contiguous set of values all present. If it contains values from two initial runs, it must include the gap value(s) between them, which are absent initially and never appear, so invalid. Thus each operation's erased values are subset of a single initial run. To erase all values in each initial run, need at least one operation per initial run. Good. This is a solid proof.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "gap_len = nxt - prev - 1" in final gap; if prev=N, gap_len=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total - no" if no includes subarrays in segments that are outside gap? We compute segments within gap using prev/nxt boundaries. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = p - last - 1" for first segment; last=prev (forbidden position before gap). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = nxt - last - 1" for last segment; nxt=forbidden position after gap. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x in gap" if there are multiple x positions, the segments between them are lengths p_j - p_{j-1} -1. We update last=p after each. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "no" sum of subarrays in segments without x; if a segment length s, number of subarrays = s*(s+1)//2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total subarrays in gap" includes subarrays that contain x and subarrays that don't; subtract no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarrays that contain x and no x-1" maybe if subarray contains x but also contains x-1 outside gap? It cannot because if contains x-1, it includes a forbidden position, so not contained in a gap. Our count only within gaps. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarray that contains x and no x-1" could start before a forbidden position and end after it but not include it? No, contiguous interval includes all positions between L and R. If forbidden position between, included. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x-1" maybe if x-1 appears multiple times, gaps are between consecutive occurrences; a valid subarray avoiding x-1 must be contained in one gap. Good.

Could choose l=2,r=4? 3 absent invalid. Could choose l=1,r=2, then {4,5}; yes. Could choose l=4,r=5, then {1,2}; yes. 2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" if S has runs but you could erase a run in one operation only if all values in run appear at least once; yes by definition. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "initial runs" proof if operations can erase a subset of a run and then combine remaining parts with other runs? Missing values between initial runs absent, so no combine. Within an initial run, if you erase a subinterval, you split it, increasing operations. So optimal is erase whole runs. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum operations at least number of initial runs" because each operation can erase values from at most one initial run. Is that true? An operation interval [l,r] is a contiguous set of values all present. If it contains values from two initial runs, it must include the gap value(s) between them, which are absent initially and never appear, so invalid. Thus each operation's erased values are subset of a single initial run. To erase all values in each initial run, need at least one operation per initial run. Good. This is a solid proof.

Thus each operation's erased values are subset of a single initial run. To erase all values in each initial run, need at least one operation per initial run. Good. This is a solid proof.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "one operation per initial run suffices" by choosing l=min run value, r=max run value; all values in between are in run by definition, so present. Erases entire run. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "runs of distinct values appearing in subarray" if values are not sorted by numeric order; run defined by numeric adjacency. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "A_i" values up to N but if value N appears and N+1 absent, N is start if N-1 absent. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "x=1" and pos[0] empty; if we delete data, pos[0] remains. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b = pos[x - 1]" for x=1; pos[0] is empty but if we had used `pos = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]`, yes index0 exists. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if not a: continue" if a is empty but b maybe nonempty; skip. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "for nxt in b:" if b is large and a is small; while may consume all a early, but still loop over remaining b. Total b iterations over all x ≤N. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "idx" after while in a gap where no a positions; start=idx, no contribution. But if a positions remain and are < nxt? while would consume them, so start<idx. If no a positions in gap, idx remains first a >= nxt. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "prev = nxt" after processing gap; if gap had no positions, prev updates. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "nxt = N + 1" after b loop; if b loop processed all b, prev last b. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "gap_len = nxt - prev - 1" in final gap; if prev=N, gap_len=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total - no" if no includes subarrays in segments that are outside gap? We compute segments within gap using prev/nxt boundaries. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = p - last - 1" for first segment; last=prev (forbidden position before gap). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = nxt - last - 1" for last segment; nxt=forbidden position after gap. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x in gap" if there are multiple x positions, the segments between them are lengths p_j - p_{j-1} -1. We update last=p after each. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "no" sum of subarrays in segments without x; if a segment length s, number of subarrays = s*(s+1)//2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total subarrays in gap" includes subarrays that contain x and subarrays that don't; subtract no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarrays that contain x and no x-1" maybe if subarray contains x but also contains x-1 outside gap? It cannot because if contains x-1, it includes a forbidden position, so not contained in a gap. Our count only within gaps. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarray that contains x and no x-1" could start before a forbidden position and end after it but not include it? No, contiguous interval includes all positions between L and R. If forbidden position between, included. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x-1" maybe if x-1 appears multiple times, gaps are between consecutive occurrences; a valid subarray avoiding x-1 must be contained in one gap. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "sentinel 0" if a subarray starts at1, before first forbidden; yes. "sentinel N+1" if ends atN; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "x=1" and b=pos[0] empty; sentinel 0 and N+1; gap whole array. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "values absent" and pos list empty; skip. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "f(L,R)" for subarray with one value, runs=1. Our count: For that value x, if x-1 absent in subarray, counted. Since subarray contains x only, x is start. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarrays containing x and not x-1" for x value maybe not minimum in S; if x appears and x-1 absent, then x is start of a run. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "runs" if S empty? no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "operation can choose l,r with l≤r" and if l=r, condition every integer from l through r appears at least once means that single value appears. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "all integers from l through r that are on the blackboard" if l,r interval includes values that appear but also values that don't? Condition prevents. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum number of operations" and "blackboard" maybe if choose interval [l,r] that contains values already erased? Condition fails because not on blackboard. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "value x appears and x-1 absent" count starts; if x-1 appears but erased earlier, could x become a start later? But we count initial runs, not dynamic. Minimum operations equals initial runs, so no need dynamic. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "initial runs" proof if operations can erase a subset of a run and then combine remaining parts with other runs? Missing values between initial runs absent, so no combine. Within an initial run, if you erase a subinterval, you split it, increasing operations. So optimal is erase whole runs. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum operations at least number of initial runs" because each operation can erase values from at most one initial run. Is that true? An operation interval [l,r] is a contiguous set of values all present. If it contains values from two initial runs, it must include the gap value(s) between them, which are absent initially and never appear, so invalid. Thus each operation's erased values are subset of a single initial run. To erase all values in each initial run, need at least one operation per initial run. Good. This is a solid proof.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "one operation per initial run suffices" by choosing l=min run value, r=max run value; all values in between are in run by definition, so present. Erases entire run. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "runs of distinct values appearing in subarray" if values are not sorted by numeric order; run defined by numeric adjacency. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "A_i" values up to N but if value N appears and N+1 absent, N is start if N-1 absent. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "x=1" and pos[0] empty; if we delete data, pos[0] remains. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b = pos[x - 1]" for x=1; pos[0] is empty but if we had used `pos = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]`, yes index0 exists. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if not a: continue" if a is empty but b maybe nonempty; skip. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "for nxt in b:" if b is large and a is small; while may consume all a early, but still loop over remaining b. Total b iterations over all x ≤N. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "idx" after while in a gap where no a positions; start=idx, no contribution. But if a positions remain and are < nxt? while would consume them, so start<idx. If no a positions in gap, idx remains first a >= nxt. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "prev = nxt" after processing gap; if gap had no positions, prev updates. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "nxt = N + 1" after b loop; if b loop processed all b, prev last b. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "gap_len = nxt - prev - 1" in final gap; if prev=N, gap_len=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total - no" if no includes subarrays in segments that are outside gap? We compute segments within gap using prev/nxt boundaries. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = p - last - 1" for first segment; last=prev (forbidden position before gap). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "seg = nxt - last - 1" for last segment; nxt=forbidden position after gap. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x in gap" if there are multiple x positions, the segments between them are lengths p_j - p_{j-1} -1. We update last=p after each. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "no" sum of subarrays in segments without x; if a segment length s, number of subarrays = s*(s+1)//2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total subarrays in gap" includes subarrays that contain x and subarrays that don't; subtract no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarrays that contain x and no x-1" maybe if subarray contains x but also contains x-1 outside gap? It cannot because if contains x-1, it includes a forbidden position, so not contained in a gap. Our count only within gaps. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "subarray that contains x and no x-1" could start before a forbidden position and end after it but not include it? No, contiguous interval includes all positions between L and R. If forbidden position between, included. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "positions of x-1" maybe if x-1 appears multiple times, gaps are between consecutive occurrences; a valid subarray avoiding x-1 must be contained in one gap. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "sentinel 0" if a subarray starts at1, before first forbidden; yes. "sentinel N+1" if ends atN; yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "x=1" and b=pos[0] empty; sentinel 0 and N+1; gap whole array. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "values absent" and pos list empty; skip. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N=300000" and "pos = [[] for _ in range(N + 1)]" maybe time to create 300k empty lists okay. Could use `defaultdict(list)` to save memory if values sparse, but list-of-lists faster. Memory okay.

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    pos = [[] for _ in range(N + 1)]
    for i in range(1, N + 1):
        v = data[i]
        pos[v].append(i)
    # optional del data
    ans = 0
    for x in range(1, N + 1):
        a = pos[x]
        if not a:
            continue
        b = pos[x - 1]
        len_a = len(a)
        idx = 0
        prev = 0
        for nxt in b:
            start = idx
            while idx < len_a and a[idx] < nxt:
                idx += 1
            if start < idx:
                gap_len = nxt - prev - 1
                total = gap_len * (gap_len + 1) // 2
                no = 0
                last = prev
                for j in range(start, idx):
                    p = a[j]
                    seg = p - last - 1
                    no += seg * (seg + 1) // 2
                    last = p
                seg = nxt - last - 1
                no += seg * (seg + 1) // 2
                ans += total - no
            prev = nxt
        nxt = N + 1
        start = idx
        while idx < len_a and a[idx] < nxt:
            idx += 1
        if start < idx:
            gap_len = nxt - prev - 1
            total = gap_len * (gap_len + 1) // 2
            no = 0
            last = prev
            for j in range(start, idx):
                p = a[j]
                seg = p - last - 1
                no += seg * (seg + 1) // 2
                last = p
            seg = nxt - last - 1
            no += seg * (seg + 1) // 2
            ans += total - no
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

I think final code ready. Let's produce final answer.</think>```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N = data[0]
    pos = [[] for _ in range(N + 1)]

    for i in range(1, N + 1):
        v = data[i]
        pos[v].append(i)

    ans = 0

    for x in range(1, N + 1):
        a = pos[x]
        if not a:
            continue

        b = pos[x - 1]
        len_a = len(a)
        idx = 0
        prev = 0

        for nxt in b:
            start = idx
            while idx < len_a and a[idx] < nxt:
                idx += 1

            if start < idx:
                gap_len = nxt - prev - 1
                total = gap_len * (gap_len + 1) // 2

                no = 0
                last = prev
                for j in range(start, idx):
                    p = a[j]
                    seg = p - last - 1
                    no += seg * (seg + 1) // 2
                    last = p

                seg = nxt - last - 1
                no += seg * (seg + 1) // 2

                ans += total - no

            prev = nxt

        nxt = N + 1
        start = idx
        while idx < len_a and a[idx] < nxt:
            idx += 1

        if start < idx:
            gap_len = nxt - prev - 1
            total = gap_len * (gap_len + 1) // 2

            no = 0
            last = prev
            for j in range(start, idx):
                p = a[j]
                seg = p - last - 1
                no += seg * (seg + 1) // 2
                last = p

            seg = nxt - last - 1
            no += seg * (seg + 1) // 2

            ans += total - no

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
